- **[RIDER 2026-09-25, Session 27 (D2 design-axis scout pair; `results/d2-scout-s26/HARVEST.md` "Proposed riders" 2 and the adjudication paragraph; `scout-F.md` §4 R-b as amended, `scout-O.md` P1 and §8; the orchestrator's `verify-orch/witt_diag_degree.py`) — the Λ-square squares the absolute point; log p enters by the arithmetic degree, but the host is one-dimensional: this entry's EXECUTABLE TEST gains a HOST-DIMENSION check.]** In Borger's Λ-algebraic geometry ("Λ-rings and the field with one element", arXiv:0906.3146; `results/d2-scout-s26/sources/O-arxiv-0906.3146.pdf`) the square of Spec Z over F₁ is printed as a definition — "if one accepts the premise of this paper, the space Spec Z ×_{Spec F₁} Spec Z must be defined to be the Witt space W*(Spec Z)" (p. 7, §1.2), W*(Spec A) = colim_n Spec W_n(A) (p. 6) — and the curve it squares is the absolute point: "The ring Z has a unique Λ-structure—each ψ_p is the identity. Under this structure, it is the initial object in the category of Λ-rings. It is therefore reasonable to denote it F₁ and call Spec Z, viewed as a Λ-space, the absolute point" (p. 8, §2.1). So End of the curve over F₁ is Hom_{Λ-rings}(Z, Z) = {id}, this entry's Hom test returns the identity alone, and III.20's item 2 has no host — Castelnuovo–Severi has nothing to act on (both scouts, at the page). Log p, however, DOES enter without hand-insertion. By "The basic geometry of Witt vectors, I: The affine case" (arXiv:0801.1691; `O-arxiv-0801.1691.pdf`), p. 10, §1.10: with E the set of all maximal ideals of Z, W^fl(Z) "consists of the ghost vectors ⟨a₁, a₂, …⟩ that satisfy a_j ≡ a_{pj} mod p^{1+ord_p(j)} for all j ≥ 1 and all primes p"; since Z is torsion-free this ring is W(Z), the classical big Witt vectors (§1.15, p. 12: with E all the maximal ideals of Z, "W agrees with the classical big Witt vector functor"). Write Γ_n: Spec Z → Spec W^fl(Z) for the n-th ghost component (the ring map a ↦ a_n) and Γ₁ ∩ Γ_n := Spec(Z ⊗_{W^fl(Z)} Z) = Spec(Z/I_n), I_n = (a₁ − a_n : a ∈ W^fl(Z)) — an ideal, being the image of a group homomorphism into Z. With the arithmetic degree deg Spec A := log #A for a finite ring A: **deg(Γ₁ ∩ Γ_n) = Λ(n) for every n ≥ 2.** Proof from (1.10) alone: for n = p^k the chained congruences a₁ ≡ a_p (mod p), a_p ≡ a_{p²} (mod p²), … give a₁ ≡ a_{p^k} (mod p), so I_{p^k} ⊆ pZ; the vector a_j = p·[p | j] is a ghost vector (for a prime q ≠ p both sides of each congruence agree; for q = p and p ∤ j it reads 0 ≡ p mod p) and gives a₁ − a_{p^k} = −p, so I_{p^k} = pZ and deg = log p; for n with two distinct prime factors q, r the ghost vectors q·[q | j] and r·[r | j] give −q, −r ∈ I_n, so I_n = Z, Γ₁ ∩ Γ_n = ∅ and deg = 0. `[dual-model check, 2026-09-25: scout O computed; the orchestrator re-derived by hand (the constant vectors are ghost vectors, so I_n = {a₁ : a_n = 0}, the tensor-product ideal) and by brute force — `verify-orch/witt_diag_degree.py`, `verify-orch/logs/witt_diag_degree_N12.log`: over the 88 869 ghost vectors on indices ≤ 12 with entries in [−6, 6], gcd{a₁ − a_n} = 2, 3, 2, 5, 1, 7, 2, 3, 1, 11, 1 at n = 2, …, 12, i.e. Λ(n) exactly at every 2 ≤ n ≤ 12; the N = 16, [−4, 4] run (`_N16.log`, 839 vectors) agrees at every n ≤ 16 except n = 11 and 13, where the box cannot hold a difference of 11 or 13 and the gcd prints 0 — an enumeration artifact, not evidence either way, as is the script's auxiliary gcd over {a₁ : a_n = 0} at n = 7, 11 (N = 12) and n = 5, 7, 10, 11, 13, 14, 15 (N = 16); the hand proof above needs no box and settles every n; re-verified from (1.10) by this stream's writer; re-derived a third time by the Opus reader — the ghost lattice on indices ≤ N has the Z-basis v_d = (d·[d | n])_{n≤N}, d ≤ N (each v_d satisfies (1.10); determinant N! = the index fixed by (1.10)), so I_n = gcd{d : d | n, d > 1}·Z for every n, and 4 000 random big-Witt vectors pushed through the ghost map w_n = Σ_{d|n} d·x_d^{n/d} give gcd(w₁ − w_n) = e^{Λ(n)} at every n ≤ 40, 11 and 13 included (results/zoo-s27/verify-O/witt_diag_O.py and its _run.log)]`. So this entry's log p clause IS satisfiable outside K1 on a printed object — by the arithmetic degree, the convention under which the closed point (p) has arithmetic degree log p (Durov, arXiv:0704.2030, p. 63: CH¹(Spec Ẑ) = log Q*₊ = Pic(Spec Ẑ), deg the "arithmetic degree"; Kapranov–Smirnov's "the degree of (p) should be log(p)", as Le Bruyn, arXiv:1304.6532, p. 2, records it), not by hand insertion; F's "integers only" clause (every finite-level intersection number an integer) was withdrawn at the adjudication as a convention difference: the integer p is #A, whose logarithm is the degree. But the host carries no second dimension: each Spec W_n(Z) is a union of copies of Spec Z — the ghost components Γ₁, …, Γ_n — glued at closed points (0801.1691 p. 5, Figure 1: "Spec W₁(A) … is two copies of Spec A glued along Spec A/pA. This is also true as schemes if we assume that A is p-torsion free"; 1006.0092 p. 5: W₁*(X) is the coequalizer of X₀ ⇉ X ∐ X, and "the space W_n*(X) can be constructed by gluing n+1 copies of X together … along their fibers modulo p, …, pⁿ"; in print as geometry in "The basic geometry of Witt vectors, II: Spaces", arXiv:1006.0092, `results/zoo-s27/prior-art/S27-1006.0092.pdf`: Prop. 16.5(d), p. 31, W_n* preserves Krull dimension — "the ghost map w_{≤n}: W_n(A) → A^{[0,n]} is integral and surjective on spectra; so the Krull dimension of W_n(A) agrees with that of A^{[0,n]}", here 1 — and Thm. 17.3, p. 39, single-ideal case, W_{n+1}*(X) as the coequalizer of S_n ×_S X ⇉ W_n*(X) ∐ X, the Witt space glued from copies of X along closed subspaces), there is no archimedean place ("since the current version of our theory says nothing about the archimedean place of Q, it is hard to imagine this succeeding without further ideas. Even so, it should be done", 0906.3146 p. 5), and the component "intersections" are zero-dimensional schemes of degree log #A ≥ 0 — no quadratic form with a sign to be negative on a fiber-orthogonal part, no Hodge-type form. **Addition to the EXECUTABLE TEST (the existing line untouched): HOST-DIMENSION check — item 2 of III.20 needs a two-dimensional host over a base below Z; a one-dimensional doubled object (one whose square is a union of copies of the base glued at closed points) can pass the log p clause and the Hom-test half and still carry no Castelnuovo–Severi step, whatever its correspondences' degrees.** Verdict on P1 unchanged: no doubled object (F: dead-end; O: instrument — a label difference only). Prior art of "the ghost components of W*(Spec Z) meet the diagonal in arithmetic degree Λ(n)": searched by this stream's writer on 2026-09-25 (Borger 0801.1691, 0906.3146 and 1006.0092 in full text; Borger–de Smit 1809.02295; Borger 1310.3013; Buium 0908.2512, 1308.5194; Connes–Consani 0903.2024, 0911.3537, 1103.4672, 1405.4527, 1502.05580, 1805.10501; Manin 0809.1564; Le Bruyn 1304.6532 and the 2011 absolute-geometry notes; Manin–Marcolli 1806.10801; Durov 0704.2030 p. 63; the nLab; six web searches — `results/zoo-s27/zoo-entries-proposed.md` finding 7) — and independently by the Opus reader (Borger's three papers re-fetched and read in full text; Connes–Consani 1103.4672 and 1502.05580; Manin 0809.1564; López Peña–Lorscheid 0909.0069; Jeffries 2311.13551; web searches — `results/zoo-s27/zoo-entries-read-O.md` §4). The case n = p IS in print, as geometry and without the words "degree" or "Λ": Borger 0801.1691 p. 5, Figure 1 (for A p-torsion free, Spec W₁(A) is two copies of Spec A glued transversely along Spec A/pA — for A = Z the ghost components Γ₁ and Γ_p meet in Spec F_p, of degree log p) and 1006.0092 p. 5 (W₁*(X) the coequalizer of X₀ ⇉ X ∐ X) `[printed: Borger 0801.1691 p. 5, Fig. 1; 1006.0092 p. 5 — the case n = p]`. The statement for every n ≥ 2 on the big Witt vectors — degree log p at every prime power p^k (not the k·log p of the printed chain "Spec W_n(A) as Spec W_{n−1}(A) glued with Spec A along Spec A/pⁿA", which measures the new copy against the whole previous union), empty intersection at every n with two distinct prime factors, i.e. deg(Γ₁ ∩ Γ_n) = Λ(n) — is NOT FOUND in print by either search: `[novelty: dual-model check, 2026-09-25: the writer's finding 7 and the Opus reader's §4, results/zoo-s27/zoo-entries-read-O.md]`. Nearest published objects (10(n)): Borger 0801.1691 p. 5, Figure 1 and 1006.0092 p. 5 (the case n = p, above); Borger's own hope, 0906.3146 p. 1 — "that Spec Z ⊗_{F₁} Z would not only make sense but be a surface bearing some kind of intersection theory"; the printed one-dimensional glued host (1006.0092 Prop. 16.5(d), Thm. 17.3); log p as an arithmetic degree in print — Smirnov's completed Spec Z with "the degree of (p) should be log(p)" (Le Bruyn, arXiv:1304.6532, p. 2, after Smirnov) and Durov's CH¹(Spec Ẑ) = log Q*₊ = Pic (arXiv:0704.2030, p. 63); and log p as a fixed-point count of Frobenius against the diagonal — Connes–Consani 1502.05580 p. 16, (15), "= Σ_{m≥1} log p g(p^m)", with "we are simply considering an intersection number", and 1103.4672 ("The value log(p) coming from the von-Mangoldt function Λ(n) corresponds to the length of the orbit in the mapping torus") — on the arithmetic site and the adèle class space, inside K1. Exact difference: on W*(Spec Z) the log p is the arithmetic degree of a scheme-theoretic intersection of two components of a printed Z-scheme, with no distribution, no trace formula and no site in its definition — and the host has no second dimension. What leaves this for C3 (HARVEST "What leaves this unit", NOT funded): Borger's named rung-1 step, 0906.3146 pp. 4–5 — translate Weil's proof for a curve S over F_q into the Λ_S-formalism and decide which steps live on S ×_{F₁^S} S = W_S*(S) (the Λ_S analog of p. 7's definition, inferred; pp. 4–5 print the Λ_S-structure and the translation task, not this equation) rather than on S ×_k S; the computation above predicts the zero-dimensional half (Frobenius components meeting the diagonal) is expressible and the quadratic-form half is not. ### IV.11 Packet indiscreteness and the X₀^E quasi-compact kill (the Deninger-substrate obstruction, faces (a) and (b)) — NEW, Session 14 (C3-r Q* stream; entered 2026-09-06, Session 16)

- **STATEMENT.** In Deninger's dynamical system for Spec Z, X₀ = X̌₀(C) ×_{Q>0} R>0 ([x-03] arXiv v4 = `fetched/x-03`), the quotient topology on the suspension is a printed DEFINITION ([x-03] p. 59, §9) — not a "reading of record" — and two theorems hold for every admissible class E of characters ([x-03] Def. 4.1, p. 27; (Tors) in force). **Face (a) — the packets are indiscrete.** Any two points of one packet Γ^E_p, a fortiori of one periodic orbit, are topologically indistinguishable in X₀: each packet and each single closed orbit is an indiscrete subspace, so no T₀ (hence no Hausdorff, metrizable, or laminated) subspace of X₀ meets a packet in two points. Holds in the p. 59 topology or any coarser one, and verbatim in the E-free systems X̌₀(S¹) ×_{Q>0} R>0 and X̌₀(C) ×_{Q>0} R>0 (it never consumes (Tors)); it is false in the strictly finer coproduct topology of [x-03] Thm. 7.10 (p. 46) — a scope boundary, not a defect, because p. 59 is Deninger's definition. **Face (b) — dissipation + finiteness, for X₀^E = X̌₀(C)_E ×_{Q>0} R>0 with E admissible, and ONLY there.** W := ρ̂₀·u is a strictly positive, lower-semicontinuous, flow-conformal weight of exponent one (W∘φ^t = e^t·W) with W = +∞ exactly on the packets; every nonempty quasi-compact subset of X₀^E invariant under the WHOLE R-action lies in ⨆_p Γ^E_p and meets only finitely many packets; hence no continuous flow-equivariant map from a nonempty quasi-compact space carrying a two-sided R-action into X₀^E meets infinitely many packets, let alone one per prime. Excluded from face (b), with counterexamples: (i) the E-free unitary Y₀ = X̌₀(S¹) ×_{Q>0} R>0 as printed ([x-03] p. 49; [x-06] p. 12) — K = Ω ∪ ⋃_p γ_p, the indiscrete orbit Ω of the trivial generic character together with one genuine periodic orbit of length log p in every packet, is a nonempty quasi-compact whole-R-invariant subset; (ii) forward-only (semiflow) invariance; (iii) any topology strictly coarser than p. 59's. **The kill, as scoped (a "(Tors)-kill").** What is proved dead is the construction of an S4 object as a quasi-compact, fixed-point-free, two-sided-R-equivariant source mapping into X₀^E, and as a subspace of X₀^E (any admissible E) — NOT "S4 is DEAD": [x-03] p. 40 poses S4 with no compactness requirement, and X₀ has no fixed points at all ([x-03] p. 3, "There are no fixed points of the flow"), so no compact source carrying archimedean fixed-point data maps equivariantly into X₀ for a one-line reason preceding face (b). C3-r Route 2 (the solenoid/foliated-space intermediate) with target X₀^E: FAILED, `failed-ledger-exhausted`; kill-criterion FIRED. Four binding record corrections: (1) p. 59's quotient topology is a definition; (2) the clause "or into the unitary system Y₀ = X̌₀(S¹) ×_{Q>0} R>0" is FALSE and STRUCK (permitted replacement Y₀^E := (X̌₀(S¹) ∩ X̌₀(C)_E) ×_{Q>0} R>0 ⊆ X₀^E); (3) "a coarser topology preserves face (b)" is inverted — face (a) survives coarsening, face (b) survives REFINING, so the two faces pin the topology to p. 59's; (4) "S4 is DEAD" restated as above. Two escapes remain: the unbuilt Arakelov compactification X̄₀ ([x-03] pp. 39–40; fixed points at u → 0⁺, ledger W5), and the E-free Y₀ — which is IV.12's separate case.
- **KILLS.** Any brief proposing a compact (or quasi-compact) sub-dynamical system of X₀ / X₀^E, or a compact source mapping into it two-sided-R-equivariantly, as the S4 object; any brief taking a Hausdorff, metrizable, or laminated subspace of X₀ that meets a packet; any hedge in either direction on the topology ("a reading of record"; "coarsening is safe"). Casualty: C3-r Route 2 with target X₀^E (Session 14).
- **EXECUTABLE TEST.** Before any construction targeting X₀, X₀^E, Y₀ or any suspension Z ×_{Q>0} R>0: (1) state the target topology (p. 59's quotient topology, or a named finer one) and check T₀/T₁ at the packets — a target that is not T₀ at the packets admits no Hausdorff subspace there; (2) state whether (Tors) is in force (E admissible): if yes, evaluate W = ρ̂₀·u on the source's image and check whether the source is quasi-compact under a two-sided R-action — dead if it must meet infinitely many packets; if no, the brief is in the E-free case and IV.12 applies; (3) state whether the brief needs archimedean fixed points — X₀ has none.

[x-03] anchors as read there: pp. 3, 27, 39–40, 46, 49, 59; [x-06] pp. 11–12.

Novelty items of the Session-14 sweep carry their own tags in the records. **BINDS: the C3-r substrate track and every brief that targets Deninger's X₀ / X₀^E or a suspension of a (Tors)-bound character space.** Entered 2026-09-06 from the Session-14 records; no new mathematics of Session 16 is in this entry.

A packet-symmetric continuum has index 0 (no nonzero translation-invariant Z-valued finitely additive measure on the clopen algebra of an infinite profinite group). The one trace that does return the Haar identity — the type II transverse-measure trace along the orbit lamination — cannot carry the arithmetic (Σ_p log p·μ_p(B_p) ≤ ν(Y) < ∞ forbids Haar probability on every packet on any compact substrate; even in t; ignores ν-null orbits). The Haar re-scoping of Q* (IV.11's kill) is NOT adopted; the index re-scoping (Q*-idx) is recorded, not adopted — unstatable on X₀ (ledger W6, W12). Three derivations: probe F (`results/c3-r/s14/dqm-F.md`), probe O (`dqm-O.md`), and the adjudicator's re-derivation (`dqm-adjudication.md` §1 V1–V3, §3–§5), adjudicated 2026-09-02 (Session 14; C3 work log "2026-09-02 (Session 14, later)"). **Novelty PARTIAL — the credit lines travel with this rider** (`results/c3-r/s14/novelty/adjudication.md` C5; dqm-adjudication `[NOVELTY — dual-model check 2026-09-03]`): the non-degeneracy-free leafwise cohomology and spectrum of a mapping torus are Kim, arXiv:1712.04181, Thms 2.1, 2.2, 3.2 — the increment is the distributional trace formula and its consequences; the index re-scoping's printed predecessor is Deninger's own Fuller-index sentence ([x-18] p. 8, [x-19] p. 10: "If the closed orbits of φ are degenerate one can define a Ruelle zeta function via Fuller indices [Fu]"); Fuller's index paper (Amer. J. Math. 89, 1967) is not on disk (adjudication D6, locate-only). **(ii) Route 1 DEAD** (ledger `results/c3-r/m2c-feasibility-ledger.md` §8, written 2026-08-26, Session 6; C3 work log Session-7 state, 2026-08-26: "Route 1 exhausted"): the verbatim ÁLKL trace formula cannot be transplanted to X₀ = X(Spec Z) as constructed — it hits OBSTRUCTED rows R1, R2, R4–R9 and R15 (R11–R13 independently): "Every analytic ingredient of ALKL (H6–H9) lacks a counterpart, the archimedean datum is absent, and the orbit count is wrong by an uncountable factor. And three term shapes mismatch INDEPENDENTLY of all smoothness (ledger §6): (1) the negative-time weights — ÁLKL's ±1 against Weil's Np^k ([Den05] p. 27, verbatim: "in the first case it is ±1 whereas in the second it is Np^k"; the leafwise return Jacobian is ≡ 1 in the Riemannian world but must be e^{ℓ(γ)} = Np arithmetically; symmetrizing the test function moves the asymmetry, never removes it); (2) ÁLKL's W_L is even in t while W_∞ is not, and the real place needs the S_Q-orbifold / ramified-double-cover mechanism ([Lei13] §4.4) — the archimedean local model is not a smooth preserved leaf; (3) the pole terms need Θ = 1 on the top leafwise cohomology, i.e. α = 1 ([Den05] pp. 26–27; [x-06] p. 8: the class of smooth compact manifolds "is too restrictive"). Math. 89 (1967)) recorded separately as the open locate-only question. Everything else reproduces: the mapping-torus trace ℓ Σ_k tr((h*)^k) δ_{kℓ} with no non-degeneracy hypothesis, the index coefficient ℓ·ind(h^k, B)·δ_{kℓ} with models 0, 2, 2 − 2g against Haar's 1, the packet-symmetric index 0, the type II transverse-measure trace and its four disqualifiers, W6/W12, and the 2026-09-02 (Session 14) adjudication date — all in ledger §13 and `dqm-adjudication.md`; Kim arXiv:1712.04181 Thms 2.1, 2.2, 3.2 per novelty C5(a) (PDF also on disk beside it). Rider (ii) reproduces verbatim at ledger §8 ("Hits OBSTRUCTED rows R1, R2, R4, R5, R6, R7, R8, R9, R15 (and R11–R13 independently) … No repair short of replacing the space itself addresses R4/R5/R6") and at §6 items 1–3 (the ±1-vs-Np^k quote at [Den05] p. 27; W_L even vs W_∞ and [Lei13] §4.4; α = 1 with [x-06] p. 8 "the class of smooth compact manifolds is too restrictive"). Verdict: **REPAIRED** (rider (i) credit line), **CONFIRMED** (rider (ii)).

### IV.12 Theorem T and the inert Y₀ witness (no Q>0-suspension of a compact base is T₁) — NEW, Session 14 (C3-r Q-b″ stream; entered 2026-09-06, Session 16)

- **STATEMENT.** **Theorem T** (Opus 5 adjudicator, `s14/y0-witness/adjudication.md` §4.3): let Z be any topological space with a Q>0-action by homeomorphisms and X = (Z × R>0)/Q>0, (z,u)·q = (qz, q⁻¹u), with the quotient topology; if z has a Q>0-orbit with compact closure (in particular z a fixed point, or Z compact), the Q>0-orbit of (z,u) is not closed in Z × R>0, so [z,u] is not a closed point: X is not T₁, hence not metrizable, hence not a foliated space in the [Den05] §7.1 sense and not a Riemann-surface lamination (proof: pick v ∉ uQ>0, q_n → u/v, a subnet q_{n_j}z → w; (w,v) is in the closure of the orbit and not in it). Corollary T1: Deninger's E-free unitary system Y₀ = X̌₀(S¹) ×_{Q>0} R>0 ([x-03] p. 49) is not T₁ (take z = π(1_η), the trivial generic character, which is Q>0-fixed); Deninger's own §8 presents Y₀ as a negative result (p. 49: "still infinite-dimensional"). Corollary T2 (the design shape): no Q>0-suspension of a COMPACT base is T₁, so the S4 object cannot be built as Z ×_{Q>0} R>0 with Z compact — independent of dimension, of E, and of all arithmetic; it also explains packet indiscreteness (IV.11 face (a)) in one line, a packet character's orbit having values in fixed finite sets of roots of unity. **The witness** (refuter F's construction, CONSTRUCTION-REPAIRED with four repairs D1–D4: a Lipschitz tube field, an explicit rank-2 normal frame in the 4-dimensional ambient, radii O(p⁻²), an E-free proof of packet indiscreteness): a compact metrizable 3-dimensional space with a flow — a 2-torus with an irrational flow plus one cabled solid torus per prime (surgery shape credited to KMNT arXiv:1906.02424 Lemma 3.2) — realizing the orbit spectrum {log p}: exactly one simple closed orbit of least period log p per prime, ε ≡ +1, no fixed points; with a REPELLING core of transverse rate ½ the leafwise return derivative is p^{1/2}·(orthogonal) and |det A_p^k| = p^k, so a [Den05] §7.5-type formula on such a piece returns T1 + T2 EXACTLY — [Den05] p. 33's printed prescription "absolute value Np^{1/2}". But **Theorem B** (verify-F, ratified and sharpened by leaf dimension): it admits NO foliated-space structure with 2-dimensional leaves (d = 3 and d = 0 excluded outright, d = 2 by the proof, d = 1 exists but is trace-formula-empty — [Den05] (32) sums over closed orbits not contained in a leaf); and **Theorem C** (verify-F, ratified): any compact source with a continuous equivariant map into the E-free Y₀ hitting infinitely many packets decomposes as Y_η ⊔ ⨆_p Y_p with Y_p clopen and Y_η mapping into the (Tors)-violating generic locus, and conversely any such decomposition admits such a map — continuity is automatic (verify-O's Theorem O-3); the map pulls back no leafwise, transverse, cohomological or conformal datum, and by Theorem T there is none on the target to transport: **the comparison map is INERT; its entire content is the bookkeeping.** Net (ledger §15): the Deninger leg is exhausted for the substrate — X₀^E killed (IV.11), Y₀ inert and not a lamination, X̄₀ unbuilt — and the orbit-spectrum-and-weights half of S4 is cheaply realizable; the unmet half is entirely topological: (a) a Riemann-surface-leaved structure surviving at the accumulation set, (b) an archimedean leaf with χ ≠ 0 in that set (the witness's limit torus has χ = 0; there are no fixed points). What remains is S4′ — Deninger's and Leichtnam's published open existence problem ([x-21] 2002 = [Den05] p. 26; [x-03] p. 40; Leichtnam 2007 Open Question 2; solved case [De02], elliptic curves) sharpened by four clauses — now amended by clause (0) (IV.15).
- **KILLS.** "Suspend a compact base by Q>0 and call it the S4 object" (any Z ×_{Q>0} R>0 with Z compact, or with a relatively compact orbit at the point meant to carry structure); "an equivariant map into Y₀ (or X₀) pulls back foliated / transverse-measure / cohomological structure" (it transports nothing, and Y₀ has none); "the orbit spectrum {log p} with weights p^{1/2} is the hard part of S4"; the phrase "first positive S4-shaped object" for the witness (struck by the Session-14 repair pass).
- **EXECUTABLE TEST.** For any suspension-shaped brief: name the base Z and the Q>0-action, then exhibit one point with relatively compact orbit (a fixed point suffices) — if one exists, the suspension is not T₁ and the brief is dead before any dynamics; if the base is only σ-compact or the flow is not the plain suspension, Theorem T does not apply (rider). For any comparison-map brief: list the structures the map is claimed to pull back and check each against Theorem C's decomposition — a map that exists for every clopen prime-indexed decomposition carries none of them.

Scope: `results/c3-r/s16/qs4prime/adjudication.md` §3.4 (the D-IV ruling: "Theorem T should not be cited again as ruling out the suspension shape in general"), §5 ("Theorem T should be re-scoped: it assumes a compact base, and Leichtnam's L is only σ-compact"), novelty row N5; `refute-adjudication.md` §3.3 (γ) and `refute-F.md` §3.3 / `refute-O.md` §3.4 (Leichtnam 2007, r3s-21 p. 2, read: L is "σ-compact", "The quotient L/Q^{+*} allows to compactify the space L × R^{+*}/Q^{+*}"; his flow φ^t(l, x) = (ψ_x^t(l), x e^{−t}) carries a renormalization factor and is not the plain suspension).

### IV.13 The closed-3-manifold length-group kill (finite-rank period group on a finite-type tangency set) — NEW, Session 16 (C3-r, Q-S4′ streams)

- **STATEMENT.** **B1** (dual-check): let M be a closed 3-manifold, F a codimension-one foliation by surfaces, φ a foliated flow whose non-transverse set L^∞ is a finite union of compact leaves (KMNT Def. 1.5(i); or ALKL transverse simplicity on a closed manifold), M₀ = M ∖ L^∞. KMNT Lemma 1.10 gives the unique closed 1-form ω on M₀ with ω|TF = 0, ω(φ̇) = 1, and Def. 1.11 the period group Λ = [ω](H₁(M₀; Z)); a closed orbit c ⊂ M₀ of least period ℓ(c) has ∫_c ω = ℓ(c); cutting M along L^∞ leaves a compact 3-manifold with boundary, so H₁(M₀; Z) is finitely generated and so is Λ; every γ_p lies in M₀ (an orbit inside a leaf gives T_xφ^ℓ|T_xF the eigenvalue 1, against clause (ii)); {log p} is Z-linearly independent (unique factorization). So S4′ clauses (i)+(ii) are unrealizable on M — the sharp form of [Den05] p. 24 Remark 3 in the presence of fixed points: the escape Deninger names there and at [x-18] p. 3 ("one must allow the flow to have fixed points") is NOT sufficient inside the KMNT/ALKL class. **B1′** (dual-model after the Opus check, three corrections): if the TANGENCY set N = {x : Y_φ(x) ∈ T_xF} is a finite union of leaves each with dim_Q H₁ < ∞, then the group Λ_{M₀} generated by the lengths of the closed orbits TRANSVERSE to F has FINITE RANK, dim_Q(Λ_{M₀} ⊗ Q) ≤ dim_Q H₁(M ∖ N; Q) < ∞. Proof: KMNT's ω exists and is locally exact on M ∖ N without transverse simplicity and without compactness of the leaves in N (verified in KMNT's own proof; with C¹ data read "closed" as "locally exact"); pass to the transverse-orientation double cover (lengths become ℓ or 2ℓ; finite rank descends); remove the leaves of N one at a time — a closure-minimal leaf is closed in the complement by Baire (the closure is a countable union of compact plaques of finitely many leaves), hence properly embedded and two-sided with a product neighborhood — and Mayer–Vietoris gives dim H₁(W ∖ L) ≤ dim H₁(W) + dim H₁(L) at each step; iterate. "Finitely generated" is NOT derivable (Z[1/2] ⊂ R has rank 1 and is not finitely generated). The kill is a dimension count: {log p} is Q-linearly independent and spans an infinite-dimensional Q-subspace of R. Closed orbits inside N are invisible to ω; under clause (ii) there are none, so with clause (i) in its "exactly" form Λ = Λ_{M₀} = ⟨log p⟩. **Necessary condition on a closed 3-manifold: rank_Q H₁(M ∖ N; Q) = ∞** — N has infinitely many leaves or a leaf of infinite topological type; "one plane/disk leaf (H₁ = 0) plus finitely many finite-type leaves" is dead independently of Theorem A (IV.14). Vacuity, as narrowed: for S4′ objects carrying clause (0) (IV.15) the set N is never a finite union of compact or finite-type leaves, so the result does not bear on them; for S4′ as posed (no clause (0)) the vacuity claim is not derivable and B1 bears on the subclass it was written for. Side note enacted with it: α = 0 is forced by any compact preserved leaf, so Deninger's printed manifold class ([x-06] p. 6) has α = 0 ([Den05] p. 24 Rem. 2 and [x-06] p. 8 force it only under "everywhere transversal" / "no fixed points").
- **KILLS.** Every construction brief on a closed 3-manifold in the KMNT (FDS³) / ALKL (transversely simple) class — finitely many compact preserved leaves — or with any finite-type tangency set; any brief whose archimedean part is "a plane leaf plus a few compact leaves"; any brief citing "the flow has fixed points" as sufficient for an infinitely generated length group; any restatement claiming "finitely generated" for the finite-type case (only finite rank is proved).
- **EXECUTABLE TEST.** Compute the tangency set N and dim_Q H₁(M ∖ N; Q) (Mayer–Vietoris along the leaves of N, as in the proof). If finite, the brief is dead before any dynamics — no closed-orbit spectrum {log p} transverse to F can exist on it. If N is infinite or contains an infinite-type leaf, record that as the price paid and route the brief to IV.14 and IV.15.
- **SOURCE.** `results/c3-r/m2c-feasibility-ledger.md` §16 S16-3 (the binding length-group statement, dual-derived; the enactment note of 2026-09-05), §16-bis S16-3 as narrowed (`refute-adjudication.md` §4), §16-ter N-2 (finite rank, tangency set, transverse orbits; enacted verbatim 2026-09-06 01:19 IST); `results/c3-r/s16/qs4prime/adjudication.md` §2 row B1 (statement, proof, and the ruling that scout F's hypothesis is right and scout O's unhypothesized conclusion is not what the argument proves); `refute-F.md` §3.4 (B1′, first derivation, F single-check); `refute-adjudication.md` §3.4 (B1′ re-derived by the adjudicator); `f1-check-O.md` §6 (Opus 5: §6.1 KMNT Lemma 1.10 read verbatim and its independence of transverse simplicity, §6.2 the tangency set, §6.3 transverse orbits and the clause-(ii) closure, §6.4 finite rank, §6.5 the double-cover and Baire repairs, §6.6 the necessary condition). KMNT = Kim–Morishita–Noda–Terashima arXiv:1906.02424, text on disk (`kmnt.txt`).

Sharpens a printed remark ([Den05] p. 24 Rem. 3); not in print in this form (Session-16 novelty ledger). **BINDS: the C3-r substrate track and every foliated-dynamics brief on a closed 3-manifold.**

B1′ — the finite-RANK generalization to a finite-type tangency set — was derived in the later refute pass, which carries no novelty ledger: `refute-adjudication.md` §8 and `f1-check-O.md` §9 record what was READ and what was DERIVED, not a prior-art search, and neither reports one for B1′. (a) **Cantwell–Conlon, *Tischler fibrations of open foliated sets*, Ann. Inst. Fourier 31 (1981) 113–135**, proof of (3.11), printed p. 127: "P(ω) is the finitely generated image of ω : H₁(K;Z) → R" — a *proved* finite-generation theorem for the period group of an **open** F-saturated set U in a closed manifold, obtained from a compact nucleus (Dippolito), where P(ω) = {t : Φ^t carries every leaf of F|U onto itself} (their §1, p. 114) is exactly Deninger's Λ and KMNT's Λ_S. Their hypotheses — every leaf of F|U dense with trivial holonomy, and "almost triviality" (a nucleus with product arms; their (3.10): U compact, or two dense ends, or transversely analytic) — are incomparable with B1′'s, and they state no rank bound and no length-spectrum consequence. (b) **Deninger arXiv:math/0204110 p. 13 Rem. 3 = math/0505354 p. 24 Rem. 3** (earliest occurrence; corollary numbered 3.5 in 2002 and 5.5 in 2005): the conclusion for the everywhere-transverse case, **asserted with no proof and no reference**, naming the fixed-point escape, repeated at [x-18] p. 3. (c) **ÁLKL**: "The abelian and torsion free group Γ_l has finite rank because π₁M¹_l ≡ π₁M̊_l ≅ π₁M_l and M_l is compact" (*Simple foliated flows* printed p. 20 = memoir §4.3.4 **printed p. 114**) together with "it easily follows that t₀ = h_l(γ₀)" for every closed-orbit period (memoir **printed p. 115**) — in the transversely simple class these two sentences give N1's conclusion in one line, though ÁLKL never state it, never confront it with {log p}, and never connect it to Deninger's escape. **New, and load-bearing:** the finite-type (possibly non-compact) tangency set with no density or holonomy hypothesis; the bound dim_Q(Λ_{M₀} ⊗ Q) ≤ dim_Q H₁(M ∖ N; Q) by iterated Mayer–Vietoris; the Baire step; **finite RANK, never "finitely generated"**; the restriction to closed orbits transverse to F; and the Q-independence kill of {log p}. **Citation integrity:** the published KMNT is **Münster J. Math. 14 (2021) 323–348**, numbering **Lemma 1.9** (= arXiv 1.10), **Definition 1.10** (= arXiv 1.11), **Remark 2.8**, **Corollary 2.9**, **Proposition 2.10** = the rank-one dichotomy (= arXiv 2.2.5); every arXiv number in this entry must carry the published number beside it. **α = 0 side note amended:** [x-06] p. 8 is ambiguous as printed, and on the natural reading Deninger asserts α = 0 (unproved) for his whole smooth-compact-manifold class **including fixed points on compact leaves** — so the program's "α = 0 is forced by any compact preserved leaf" *proves* a printed assertion instead of filling a gap print leaves open. Standing SPONSOR-FETCH bearing here: Kopei 2011 (Abh. Hamburg 81, 141–189); Farber, *Topology of Closed One-Forms*, §2.1 and Calegari, *Foliations and the Geometry of 3-Manifolds*, §9.3 — the two sources KMNT themselves cite for the rank-one characterization (published p. 331), neither opened.

### IV.14 Theorem A: α = 1 versus fixed points on compact laminations (the archimedean lamination is hyperbolic and measure-null) — NEW, Session 16 (C3-r, Q-S4′ streams)

- **STATEMENT.** Hypotheses, exactly as fixed in `f1-check-O.md` §4.4: X a compact foliated space whose leaves are Riemann surfaces (Candel's compact Riemann-surface lamination); g a leafwise metric in the conformal class, of Candel's regularity class C_l^∞ (leafwise smooth with all leafwise derivatives continuous in all variables, Candel §1.1–1.2 pp. 491–493 — Deninger's A^•_F asks only transverse continuity, [x-20] p. 30 Warning, so "continuous leafwise metric" does not license Candel's theorems; (A), (B), (C1), (C4) need only continuity of g); φ a foliated flow (a JOINTLY continuous R-action by homeomorphisms mapping leaves to leaves, smooth along leaves, [x-20] 7.3); N the union of the preserved leaves (closed, saturated, flow-invariant). **(A)** Every positive holonomy-invariant transverse measure µ concentrated on N is flow-invariant, φ^t_*µ = µ — the transverse map induced by φ^t agrees with plaque-chain holonomy ON N, and holonomy invariance quantifies over all Borel sets. **(B)** If φ^{t*}[λ_g] = e^t[λ_g] in H̄²_F(X) — Deninger's α = 1, [x-20] p. 27 = Leichtnam 2013 (15) — there is no nonzero FLOW-INVARIANT holonomy-invariant transverse measure (the Ruelle–Sullivan current C_µ is bounded for the C⁰ seminorm, one of the seminorms of Deninger's Fréchet topology, [x-20] pp. 14, 29, hence descends to reduced cohomology; then (e^t − 1)m = 0 with 0 < m < ∞ the total g-area mass); with (A), none concentrated on N. One-dimensionality of H̄²_F is not used. **(C)** Under (B)'s hypothesis: (C1) N contains no compact leaf; with g in Candel's class, (C2) N contains no euclidean leaf and (C3) every preserved leaf is a hyperbolic Riemann surface (N is a compact Riemann-surface lamination with no invariant transverse measure — Candel Cor. 4.2, p. 497); (C4) for every holonomy-invariant transverse measure µ of X and every regular transversal T, µ(T ∩ N) = 0 — equivalently the Ruelle–Sullivan measure gives N measure zero and N contributes nothing to χ_Co(F, µ). **(D)** If in addition there is a fixed point x_∞ with leaf L and Deninger's conformality (31) ([x-20] p. 27: g(T_xφ^t v, T_xφ^t w) = e^t g(v, w)) holds on TF|L, NO OBJECT exists: φ^t|L is a similarity of ratio e^{t/2}, orientation-preserving (det continuous, nonvanishing, +1 at t = 0), hence holomorphic, with |(φ^t)′(x_∞)| = e^{t/2} (the conformal factor cancels at a fixed point); Schwarz–Pick excludes hyperbolic L, the automorphism groups exclude tori and C^*, a Möbius one-parameter group's second fixed point (reciprocal derivative) excludes Ĉ by (31)-along-L alone, and L ≅ C is excluded by (C2) — the only place (0-coh) + compactness + Candel enter. **Corollary A.1:** under (0-fix) + (0-coh) the flow is NOT conformal along the archimedean leaf for any t ≠ 0 (Deninger's "(31) probably too strong", reported at Leichtnam 2007 p. 11, is "inconsistent"); its second half — χ = +1 ⇒ L conformally the disk — holds only under Reading (iii)′ (L of finite topological type, χ = 2 − 2g − n; IV.15). **GUARD** ([De02] = [x-20] §7.7 Example, pp. 34–36, read at source): a COMPACT object satisfying (31) globally with α = 1, all leaves conformally C, carrying a nonzero holonomy-invariant transverse measure — and with NO fixed point (p. 35: the flow "is everywhere transverse to the leaves of F and in particular has no fixed points"). It is consistent with (A)–(D) and pins their scope: (C2) forbids euclidean leaves only INSIDE N; (B) forbids only FLOW-INVARIANT measures; (D) is false without the fixed point. **Theorem A must never be paraphrased as "α = 1 is impossible on a compact space": what is impossible is α = 1 together with a fixed point on a leaf along which (31) holds.** Program consequences: "conformal to C, χ = +1" for the archimedean leaf is STRUCK (it rested on [Den05] p. 33 Rem. 7 — an expectation that presupposes a transverse measure, names no particular leaf, and for K ≠ Q predicts hyperbolic leaves); the archimedean part of any S4′ + clause-(0) object is a compact saturated lamination of hyperbolic preserved leaves with µ(T ∩ N) = 0 for every invariant µ; and A-III ("a holonomy-invariant transverse measure necessarily exists") FELL — the only printed route, Candel via a non-hyperbolic leaf, is closed inside N by (C3), while a non-preserved non-hyperbolic leaf does produce a measure and occurs in [De02].
- **KILLS.** Any brief adding Deninger's (31) globally — or "each φ^t acts on each leaf by a biholomorphism" — to a compact object with an archimedean fixed point under α = 1; any brief placing clause (iv)'s transverse measure on the archimedean lamination N, making it flow-invariant, or letting N contribute to χ_Co(F, µ); any brief with a compact or euclidean archimedean (preserved) leaf under α = 1; "a transverse measure necessarily exists" (existence is an axiom, Leichtnam 2008 5] / 2013 5]); any brief deriving χ = +1 for the archimedean leaf by Poincaré–Hopf on a non-compact leaf. NOT killed (guard): α = 1 on a compact space as such; euclidean non-preserved leaves; nonzero non-flow-invariant measures — [De02] is the witness for all three.
- **EXECUTABLE TEST.** Write the brief's conformality hypothesis: (31) global? (31) along the archimedean leaf? (15) = (0-coh) only? Locate every fixed point and its leaf L. If (31) holds on TF|L at a fixed point on a compact space with α = 1 — dead by (D). Otherwise compute whether the brief's transverse measure would be flow-invariant on N (by (A) it is, if concentrated there): nonzero there — dead by (B); check that no preserved leaf is compact or euclidean; check that g's regularity (Candel's class) is stated wherever Candel is invoked and that Reading (iii)′ is stated wherever χ of a non-compact leaf is used.

### IV.15 Specification rider: S4′ carries clause (0) (the four clauses do not imply the fixed-point source property) — NEW, Session 16 (C3-r, Q-S4′ streams)

- **STATEMENT.** S4′ as stated in Session 14 (ledger §15) had four clauses: (i) exactly one simple closed orbit of length log p per prime; (ii) return derivative p^{1/2}·O (Leichtnam 2013 (14)); (iii) a preserved leaf of nonzero Euler characteristic in the accumulation set; (iv) a transverse measure. **They do NOT imply the fixed-point source property.** In [Den05] the fixed-point form (p. 33 Fact: "For fixed points, ε_x = 1 is automatic and we have: T_xφ^t = e^{t/2}O_t for O_t ∈ SO(T_xF)") is a one-line corollary of the GLOBAL conformality (31) ("in the situation of the preceeding remark", pp. 31–33) and has no other source; Leichtnam 2013 (r3s-20 §4.1) prints it as the separate axiom 3] a) (13) beside 3] b) (14) and 4] (15); Leichtnam 2008 (§5.1) prints only the fixed-point form (12). Counter-configuration (verified): a compact preserved leaf L ≅ S² on which φ^t|L is the flow of z ↦ e^{t/2}z on Ĉ (source at 0, sink at ∞, no closed orbits) violates nothing in (i)–(iv). **S4′ is AMENDED by clause (0) = (0-fix) + (0-coh):** (0-fix) — the flow has a fixed point x_∞ (one per archimedean place) lying in clause (iii)'s leaf, and at every fixed point e^{−t/2}T_xφ^t|T_xF ∈ SO(T_xF) (Leichtnam 2013 (13) = Deninger's Fact); (0-coh) — H̄²_F(X) ≅ R·[λ_g] and φ^{t*}[λ_g] = e^t[λ_g] (Deninger's definition of α = 1, [Den05] p. 27 = Leichtnam 4] (15)). NOT added: (0-glob) = (31), nor "each φ^t acts on each leaf by a biholomorphism" — with a fixed point on a compact space either is inconsistent with (0-coh) (IV.14 (D), Corollary A.1); Leichtnam already replaced (31) by (15) in 2007 on Deninger's advice (r3s-21 p. 11) and repeats the caution in 2013 Comment 6. **What the archimedean term actually FORCES at a fixed point** (`f1-check-O.md` §7): ε_x = sign det(1 − T_xφ^t|T_xF) on the LEAF ([x-20] p. 31 Rem. 7.6(2), the normal direction split off into |1 − e^{κ_x|t|}|⁻¹); the explicit formula's archimedean W_p ([x-20] p. 10) has κ_p = −1 (complex) or −2 (real), both NEGATIVE, so matching forces ε_x = +1 on R>0 AND det(T_xφ^t|T_xF) = e^t on R<0 (Deninger's p. 32 "fits perfectly" is sufficiency; the sign of κ_p makes it necessary). Writing T_xφ^t|T_xF = exp(tB): tr B = 1 and ε_x = +1 together force both eigenvalues into the open right half-plane — a leafwise hyperbolic SOURCE; the saddle diag(2, −1) has ε_x = −1 and is EXCLUDED, the sink is excluded by tr B = 1; neither condition nor both forces SO(2) (B = diag(1/4, 3/4) is a non-conformal source with tr B = 1 and ε_x = +1) — **SO(2) is an axiom, or a theorem under (31).** **Reading (iii)′ (finite type):** clause (iii)'s "nonzero Euler characteristic" is read as "L of finite topological type and χ(L) = 2 − 2g − n"; then χ = +1 forces L ≈ R² and, hyperbolic under (0-coh), L ≅ D; for an infinite-type leaf χ is undefined and clause (iii) says nothing; χ = +1 is not derivable from (0-fix) plus "one fixed point per leaf" (Poincaré–Hopf needs control at the ends). **Clause (iv) restated** (ledger §16-bis S16-6; verbatim clause in `refute-adjudication.md` §5.5): existence is an axiom; under clause (0) the measure is never flow-invariant, is scaled by the flow with modulus exactly e^{−t}, the return map at γ_p scales it by 1/p (Leichtnam 2007 Lemma 6, Prop. 2), it gives every transversal's intersection with N measure zero, and its ALKL parenthetical is void as a justification (ALKL's trace formula never applies to an S4′ + clause-(0) object, S16-5 (α)) while its rationale — the χ_Co(F, µ)δ₀ term of [Den05] (24) p. 23 and Rem. 6 p. 33 — stands. Also: clause (ii) forces every γ_p transverse to F.

### IV.18 The Sector-I confinement theorem (strip-positive cone certificates reach only a de la Vallée Poussin layer, at every bandwidth) — NEW, Session 21 (C2 mandatory repair 1)

- **STATEMENT.** Let Σ_L be the strip-positive cone (w ∈ C²_c, w ≥ 0, ŵ ≥ 0, supp w ⊂ [−L, L]; test g = w/cosh(u/2)), B(w) = 2ŵ(0) + ∫(ŵ ∗ μ₀)A the pole-plus-archimedean budget, μ_y(ξ) = 2cos(πy)cosh(πξ)/(cosh 2πξ + cos 2πy) the depth kernel (mass 1, peak 1/sin(πδ), δ = ½ − y = 1 − β), Ψ(s) = Σ_{γ>0} 1/cosh(π(s − γ)) the sech-smoothed density of ζ's on-line zeros. **THEOREM R1** (`confinement-note.md` §1–§6): (1) B(w) = Z(w) + P(w) with Z ≥ 2∫ŵΨ ≥ 0 and P ≥ 0, from the formal explicit formula `EF_lit_zetaZeroConfig` (`Zeta23/WeilEF/Main.lean` 270) at g ∈ C²_c and Λ ≥ 0 (axiom P, consumed exactly once). (2) If 2[μ_y(t − s) + μ_y(t + s)] ≤ Ψ(s) + Ψ(−s) for all s ≥ 0 — local content **2/sin(πδ) ≤ Ψ(t)** — then 4(ŵ ∗ μ_y)(t) ≤ B(w) for every w ∈ Σ_L and every L: no cone certificate excludes a zero at (t, ½ − δ) at any bandwidth. (3) For t ≥ 28 the hypothesis of (2) follows from 2/sin(πδ) + ε₃(δ) ≤ min_{|s−t|≤4}Ψ(s), ε₃(δ) = 4.03 sin(πδ)e^{−4π} ≤ 1.41·10⁻⁵; the gap condition it also needs (all consecutive zero ordinates within 7.5) is a THEOREM for ζ (Theorem G, from Hasanalizade–Shen–Wong arXiv:2107.06506v1 Cor. 1.2/1.4/(5.6) and Trudgian arXiv:1208.5846v2 (2.5), read at the page, plus the first 1900 zeros). (4) Where min_{|s−t|≤4}Ψ ≥ (1 − ε)ℓ/2π > 2, ℓ = log(t/2π), every point with 1 − σ ≥ (4/ℓ)(1 + ε + O(ε²) + O(ℓ⁻²)) is uncertifiable: **the reach lies in the layer 1 − σ < (4 + o(1))/log t** (under RH ε → 0, Littlewood's S(t) bound — recalled, not load-bearing). (5) Unconditionally, from HSW Cor. 1.2 via a window count: every point with 1 − σ ≥ (66.41 + o(1))/log t is uncertifiable for t ≥ e^{377}. (6) Parameter pin: the orbit share of p(u)q(tu) beyond |u| > L′ is ≤ 16p(0)‖q‖_∞e^{−δL′}/δ — bandwidth pinned at ≍ 1/δ by cosh(yu)/cosh(u/2) ≤ 2e^{−δ|u|}. (7) Sharpness: de la Vallée Poussin's element e^{−a|u|}cosh(u/2)(3 + 4cos tu + cos 2tu) lies in the cone (identity proved from the formal input by approximation) and certifies 1 − β ≥ 0.1436/(5ℓ + 1.53 + 13.5/t + 34/t²) > 1/(34.82 log t): the reach is exactly a dlVP-shaped band between c/log t and C/log t. **Correction of record (D1):** the pricing's hypothesis "1/sin(πδ) ≤ Ψ" (`PRICING-next-unit.md` §1.2, C2 line 81) is off by a factor 2 — a pair puts two zeros at height t — and its literal form over all real s is unsatisfiable (it fails at s = −t); the layer constant is 4, not 2; the pricing's edge table at t = 10⁶ is superseded by `confinement-note.md` §7 Table E (the theorem is vacuous where Ψ < 2, i.e. at most points below t ≈ 1.8·10⁶; first nonvacuous edges δ*·ℓ = 3.9–6.7 at 10⁷–10⁸).
- **KILLS.** Any brief claiming that a strip-positive (cone / positivity-class / de la Vallée Poussin–Landau–Heath-Brown-type) first-order certificate, at any bandwidth, yields a zero-free point at a height and depth where the theorem's density hypothesis holds — i.e. wherever 2/sin(πδ) + ε₃(δ) ≤ min_{|s−t|≤4} Ψ(s), which is 1 − σ ≳ 4/log t at mean density (clause 4; under RH at every large height) and 1 − σ ≳ 66.41/log t unconditionally for t ≥ e^{377} (clause 5); at heights where Ψ(t) < 2 (most points below t ≈ 1.8·10⁶) the theorem is silent and this screen does not fire [KILLS scope per `check-O.md` §12 item 1] — in particular C2's original numerics deliverable "V > 1 outside the classical region" (C2 line 70, the killer's major) — and any brief pricing the cone's depth sensitivity as anything but 1/sin(πδ) against the local on-line density. Reading for the field: Sector I is a layer sector; the interior of the strip belongs to Sector II (signed tests) or to nothing yet.
- **EXECUTABLE TEST.** Given a proposed cone certificate at (t, ½ − δ): compute Ψ on |s − t| ≤ 6 from the zeros (mpmath.zetazero; `verify/confinement_edge.py`); if 2/sin(πδ) ≤ Ψ(s) − ε₃ pointwise (or the window-min form holds) the certificate cannot exist — return the brief with the identity B = Z + P and the domination line. Given a proposed "deeper" cone element: check its orbit share against 16p(0)‖q‖e^{−δL′}/δ beyond L′ = 2/δ (clause 6) and its budget against clause 7's ledger. Negative control (V.4): the de la Vallée Poussin element must and does certify δ < 0.0287/log t (`verify/dlvp_constant.py`), and the theorem must and does say nothing at t = 10⁶ where Ψ < 2 (Table E).

### IV.16 The Duminy endset kill of the semiproper archimedean leaf (one end versus a Cantor set of ends) — NEW, Session 19 (C3-r, Q-S4⁗ streams)

- **STATEMENT.** Let (M, 𝓕, φ) satisfy the contract of digest §I in its manifold case — M a closed 3-manifold, 𝓕 a C² codimension-one foliation, transversely oriented, M oriented (the hypotheses of the printed instruments; S19-7), φ a C¹ foliated flow with (i)–(iv) and clause (0) — so that (D1)–(D6) hold: the archimedean leaf L ≅ D (Reading (iii)′, χ = +1) has one end, and L̄ contains an exceptional minimal set K (Theorem O-5). **Then L is not semiproper: L has no proper side; equivalently (Candel–Conlon I Lemma 5.3.2, p. 133) L is not a border leaf of any open saturated set.** Proof: Duminy's Theorem 1.1 as printed by Cantwell–Conlon (Warsaw 2002, p. 225 — "If the leaf F of 𝓕 is semiproper and if it accumulates on the exceptional LMS X, then ℰ^X(F) is homeomorphic to the Cantor set") with F = L, X = K would make the set ℰ^K(L) of ends of L asymptotic to K a Cantor set; ℰ(L) is one point, that point is asymptotic to K (K ⊆ lim L), so ℰ^K(L) is one point. **Consequences, all printed:** L is not proper (a proper leaf is semiproper, pp. 225–226); if L is at infinite level this is also Candel–Conlon I Lemma 8.3.23 (p. 195); the route "(S) ⟹ (b-NO)" of digest §I is dead for every object. **What survives is a non-semiproper one-ended leaf in an exceptional LMS (levels 0 or k ≥ 1) or at infinite level** — the first two are the negation of the Cantwell–Conlon conjecture (Warsaw p. 226; Hurder 5.4 in the minimal case), the third is unconjectured in print; the kill of the object would need K (or Q) Markov (Hurder 5.5, open) or real analyticity (Candel–Conlon I p. 199, outside the contract). In C¹ nothing here applies.
- **KILLS / RETURNS.** Any brief on a closed 3-manifold claiming (b-NO) "by Duminy" for an S4′ object; any brief whose archimedean leaf is asserted proper, semiproper, a border leaf of an open saturated set, or a leaf "with a product side"; any brief asserting that a disk/plane leaf accumulating on an exceptional LMS must be semiproper (Example 8.3.20 + Lemma 8.3.23 are the printed counter-configuration); any brief citing Hurder's Problem 5.4 or the Warsaw p. 226 conjecture as a theorem, or citing Warsaw p. 258 for ends; any brief killing the infinite-level branch by reading Candel–Conlon's "foliated product" as a product foliation (Definition 5.2.2, p. 126 — an interval bundle); any brief applying Duminy or the theory of levels to a C¹ foliation or without transverse orientation. **NOT killed (guard):** the object of the contract itself — the manifold face is OPEN-NAMED, the lamination face is open and unnamed; a brief constructing either is RETURNED under standing order 6 as "open in print", not refuted.
- **EXECUTABLE TEST.** (1) Check the brief's regularity and orientation hypotheses against Warsaw p. 225 and Candel–Conlon I p. 187; below C² or without transverse orientation, stop — no instrument applies. (2) Locate the archimedean leaf L, its conformal type (Reading (iii)′) and its endset; locate an exceptional LMS in L̄ (Theorem O-5 needs (0-coh) and clause (iii)). (3) Ask whether L has a proper side — i.e. whether the brief exhibits a transverse arc meeting L only once (Lemma 5.3.2). If yes: dead, by Duminy against one end. If no: read off the level of L (Candel–Conlon I Definition 8.3.6) — level 0 or finite: the brief claims a counterexample to Warsaw p. 226 / Hurder 5.4, RETURN as "open problem in print"; infinite level: RETURN as "unconjectured in print" and record the printed structure (Lemmas 8.3.22–8.3.23). (4) If the brief claims K Markov, demand the one-sided subshift presentation of Γ|K (Hurder p. 10) — that is Problem 5.5.
- **SOURCE.** `results/c3-r/s19/qs4quad/adjudication.md` §§1–3 (pages re-derived: Warsaw pp. 225–228, 257–258; Candel–Conlon I pp. 115, 126, 129, 133, 176, 187, 189, 191, 193–197, 199; Hurder p. 10; Ghys 1999 §3.2), §2 D1–D9 (the rulings), §4 (ledger §16-sexies); `scout-F.md` §§1–5 and `scout-O.md` §§1–5 (the two independent derivations); `results/fetch-r4/a01-12-warsaw.md` §3.1 (the ingest read of Duminy); digest `results/c3-r/s19/insights-digest.md` §G, §I (the contract), §J (the twelve sentences); ledger §16-ter §8.4 block (the question as posed).
- **STATUS.** program-adjudicated — dual-model (Scout F on Fable 5.1 and Scout O on Opus 5, independently, neither reading the other; re-derived at the page by the Fable 5.1 adjudicator). The kill is PRINTED (Duminy, Cantwell–Conlon 2002) applied to PROGRAM structure ((D4), (D5)); it is NOT a kill of the object and NOT new mathematics — it is the literature decision standing order 6 asked for. **BINDS: the C3-r substrate track and every foliated-dynamics brief on a closed 3-manifold.** Prior art: the argument "a semiproper leaf of an exceptional minimal set has a Cantor set of ends, so it is not a plane" is the printed content of Duminy's theorem itself (Hurder p. 10: "the semiproper leaves of K must have a Cantor set of ends"); nothing here is claimed as novel.
- **[RECORD CORRECTION 2026-09-10 — Session 20, Round-5 ingest enacted (`FETCH-RESPONSE-ROUND5.md` rows 4–6; `results/fetch-r5/a03.md`).]** In STATEMENT, "the third is unconjectured in print" and in EXECUTABLE TEST (3), "infinite level: RETURN as 'unconjectured in print'", read: the infinite-level configuration — a one-ended plane leaf at infinite level with an exceptional minimal set in its closure — is PRINTED as realizable in every closed 3-manifold (C^∞, transversely orientable: Cantwell–Conlon, Topology 26 (1987), proof of Thm 5.1 p. 283; CMH 73 (1998), Ex. 10.4 p. 333), and no source conjectures anything about its ends; it carries none of the contract's clauses (0)–(D6). So a brief in branch (P∞) is RETURNED as "printed topological configuration, unconjectured as to ends, with the contract's flow/measure/hyperbolicity clauses unaddressed in print" — still under standing order 6, still not refuted. Also binding here: the printed source of "the generic leaf has one end or a Cantor set of ends" is Cantwell–Conlon CMH 73 (1998) Theorem 9.1 p. 329 (residual, not measure), and r5-10 (Math. Ann. 265) is NOT the source of the p. 199 real-analytic remark. Nothing in KILLS/RETURNS or the NOT-killed guard changes.

Math. 70 (r5-07b) Theorems 4 and 8) carries "totally proper" or "growth dominated by a polynomial", both forcing finite level; the word "end" does not occur in r5-03 §5 (pp. 198–199); CMH 73 Thm 9.3 (p. 330) is residual only (`results/fetch-r5/a02.md` §3.4). It constrains a named leaf L ⊂ K only if L ∈ G and says nothing about a leaf outside X (the last clause is a03's flagged inference). **Theorem 9.1 is the printed home of "one end or a Cantor set of ends" — cite it instead of Warsaw p. 227** (`a03.md` §3.1(a),(b),(f)). **(§F 9) Dippolito reinstates nothing:** his Theorem 3 (Annals 107 (1978), pp. 417–418, "Generalized Reeb Stability": a leaf with a proper side is semistable or has unbounded holonomy on that side) is a dichotomy needing neither C² nor orientability (𝓕 of class 𝒞^r, r ≥ 0, with smooth leaves; a transverse 𝒞^∞ one-dimensional foliation fixed); nothing in Dippolito relates a boundary leaf of the completion to its endset, to C², to the minimal sets it accumulates on, or to levels; "border leaf" is not his vocabulary ("boundary leaf of the completion", "sides", "semistable" are — never merge with Candel–Conlon's Thm 5.3.4) (`a03.md` §3.2(b),(c),(d)). ### IV.17 The fractional-mark integrality barrier (the 5/6 corner is a theorem about integer marks; every convexified reading of the two-moment LP class is unsound) — NEW, Sessions 7–8 (A4 no-go paper; entered 2026-09-10, Session 21)

- **STATEMENT.** In the A4 gate's marked-configuration world (`results/a4-no-go/paper.md` §2.4: N-periodic columns of on-line atoms with integer marks m_i ≥ 1 and conjugate off-line pairs, mass N per period; laws = probability mixtures of columns) the whole 5/6 baseline and its simple-fraction sibling rest on ONE per-configuration inequality, the **master inequality** (paper §4.2) **(MI) F1(c) ≥ 3M(c) − 2N_d(c)** — F1 the bandwidth-one Frobenius row, M the total mark mass, N_d the number of distinct zeros — because (MI) per configuration gives, for every law with mass N and E[F1] ≤ (4/3)N(1 + ε), both corners E[N_d]/N ≥ 5/6 − (2/3)ε and E[p1] ≥ 2/3 − (4/3)ε (`pair-channel.md` Lemma 1.1). **(MI) is FALSE for fractional marks, at the baseline itself:** mass N of mark-4/3 atoms on the (N+1)-site grid of the grid-Parseval theorem (paper Thm 3.9 = `theorems.md` Thm 1.2, F1 = Σ m² there) has F1 = (3/4)N·(16/9) = (4/3)N, budget-tight, and N_d = (3/4)N < (5/6)N, while (MI) would demand F1 ≥ 3N − (3/2)N = (3/2)N (paper §2.4, "with fractional marks even the 5/6 baseline is false"; revision record R1-m7). **And in the pair channel** (paper Prop. 4.5 = `pair-channel.md` Prop. 3.1, exact): the vacancy lattice (unit atoms on grid sites g₁…g₆₄) plus one pair at the hole g₀ with depth d > 0 and REAL mark μ > 0 has F1 − S2 = 2μ²ā(2d)² − 4μ(ā(d)² − 1) (ā(x) := ψ(ix) ≥ 1), NEGATIVE for every 0 < μ < 2(ā(d)² − 1)/ā(2d)² — the pair harvests the hole's missing coupling linearly in μ and pays only quadratically; numeric check at (d, μ) = (0.25, 0.05): F1 − S2 = −3.520·10⁻², reproduced to 10⁻¹²; for integer marks the same family is safe by ā(d)² − 1 ≤ (ā(2d) − 1)/2. **Reading (paper §4.2):** this is the textbook integrality gap — the fractional relaxation of the marked-configuration program has a strictly smaller optimum than the integer program, "so a bound proved for the relaxation is simply not a bound for the problem"; the channel is "invisible to every convexified or soft-positivity analysis"; the decision LP relaxed nothing about marks (its columns are explicit integer configurations), which is why its converged pricing found no pair column. **Integrality is consumed through exactly two devices** (`pair-channel.md` §3, Reading): the per-zero slack (m − 1)(m − 2) ≥ 0 (`theorems.md` Lemma 2.2(a), equality iff m ∈ {1, 2}) and the mark-1 floor m² ≥ m inside the cell-capacity counts. Every closure and every baseline argument in this class must name where it consumes one of them. **Second paragraph — the proved coverage of the pair-interference channel** (same theorem family; paper §4.3, `pair-channel.md` §6, with the dated revision of 2026-08-27): (MI) holds for every configuration with exactly one pair (arbitrary atoms, integer marks, positions) at pair depth d ≤ 0.45, i.e. dimensionless w = 2πd ≤ 2.8 — the gate's entire pair-depth family including both deep probes, with ≥ 34% capacity margin on the family (Thm 4.6); for every configuration all of whose pair depths satisfy w ≤ 0.82, unconditionally, and for w ≤ 0.98 modulo one isolated cell-crowding cap whose ledger constant is interval-certified, sup Sgen2 ≤ 0.98465 < 1 on (0, 0.156]² by per-cell rigorous enclosures (`verify/o1_crowding_interval.py`; Thm 4.7, Session 8); on the sliver w ∈ (0.98, 1] the ledger inequality FAILS — interval-certified Sgen2(0.159, 0.159) ≥ 1.01405 > 1 — so the ledger route claims nothing there and coverage is the re-run crowd-plus-sea attacks (min F1 − T ≥ +17.46) plus the backstop; for equal-depth pair-only configurations F1 ≥ S2 + 2Σ_p m_p² at every depth (Thm 4.8); and unconditionally, for every admissible configuration, N_d ≥ (4/9)(3M − F1), so E[N_d]/N ≥ (8/9)(5/6 − (2/3)ε) = 20/27 − (16/27)ε — the maximal conceivable pair advantage on the corner is ≤ 5/54 ≈ 0.0926 (Thm 4.9, the 8/9-strength spectral backstop). The earlier full-family crowding ratio 0.9775 was an artifact of the 0.004 evaluation grid and was withdrawn by the dated revision (`pair-channel.md` header; `o1-n128-report.md` §1, §5). Residue, honestly enumerated: multi-pair columns at the deep-probe depths w ∈ {1.5, 2} and multi-pair columns with a depth in (0.98, 1] rest on adversarial numerics plus the backstop (paper §8.3, O1–O2). No bite exists anywhere: the channel could only ever have produced one (a pair-assisted violation would be cut by pricing, not absorbed), and it produces none.
- **KILLS.** Any brief that relaxes marks to reals — or uses a continuous-density adversary, a convex-hull-of-moments argument, or "LP duality shows …" for the pair channel — and reads off a bound for the integer-mark class; any baseline or closure argument for the two-moment class that names no integrality step; any brief treating the 5/6 corner as a positivity of the quadratic form rather than a lattice-point property of it; any brief claiming a pair-channel bite outside the covered regimes without first beating the 8/9 backstop. Casualty: the audit's open pair-interference finding (`results/a4-m2-gate/AUDIT.md` §5 finding 4) — discharged for the closure's regimes, not by convexity but by integrality.
- **EXECUTABLE TEST.** (1) Run the brief's claimed relaxation or convexified bound against the mark-4/3 atom law (all atoms of mark 4/3 on the grid, mass N; F1 = Σ m² = (4/3)N, N_d = (3/4)N): it must FAIL to certify F1 ≥ 3M − 2N_d — if it "certifies" it, the argument is unsound. (2) Run it against Prop. 4.5's family at (d, μ) = (0.25, 0.05) and check that F1 − S2 = −3.520·10⁻² is reproduced (a correct relaxation-bound must fail there too). (3) In every closure or baseline argument, locate the line where integrality is consumed — the (m − 1)(m − 2) slack or the m² ≥ m floor — and return the brief if there is none. ### IV.19 The Kronecker sharpness of the pointwise prime-sum wall (no height-uniform prime-side estimate certifies a first-order datum's sign at a chosen height; the detector is an evaluation, and its wall is the term count) — NEW, Session 22/23 (C2 mandatory repairs 4 and 6)

- **STATEMENT.** For the first-order datum W_Z(f_{t,L}) = Σ_Z ĝ(γ) at the test f_{t,L} = i(B_L)′e^{−itu}, g = f ⋆ f̃ (B the Gevrey-2 bump; `results/c2-m2/separation-note.md` §0.1), the explicit formula `EF_lit_zetaZeroConfig` (`Zeta23/WeilEF/Main.lean` 270; `ExplicitFormula.lean` 70–74 `literatureRHS`) writes W_ζ(f_{t,L}) = ĝ(i/2) + ĝ(−i/2) + ARCH_ζ(t, L) − P_X(g_t) with the FINITE prime sum P_X(g_t) = Σ_{n≤X}Λ(n)n^{−1/2}(g(log n) + g(−log n)) = Σ_{n≤X}w_n cos(t log n), X = e^{L}, w_n = 2Λ(n)n^{−1/2}a(log n) t-independent. **THEOREM (E)** (Bohr 1913; Turán, *Acta Sci. Math. Szeged* 21 (1960) 311–318, Lemma p. 313, for {log p}, with an effective window; Weber, *Unif. Distrib. Theory* 4 (2009) 97–116 = arXiv:0806.3990, Theorem 1 and (3.17)–(3.18), in general and effective; proof reproduced for this coefficient system in `m6-rung1-note.md` §6, non-effective — ℚ-independence of {log p} by unique factorization, Weyl's mean values uniform in T, Fejér's theorem on the torus, a trigonometric minorant of a bump at the torus maximizer): for every T and every H ≥ H₀(ε, L), the supremum of P_X(g_t) over t ∈ [T, T + H] lies within ε of the torus supremum Σ_p max_θ φ_p(θ), φ_p(θ) = Σ_k w_{p^k}cos(kθ), and likewise the infimum; so sup_t|P_X(g_t)| = sup_𝕋|Φ|, which is the ℓ¹ norm Σ_n|w_n| up to the prime-power alignment defect (2.4 % at L = 10, 0.08 % at L = 20). Instance (`verify/kronecker_instance.py`): sup_t|P| = 1.422174 at L = 10 (2 532 terms) and 12.529890 at L = 20 (25 617 272 terms), against actual values |P| ≤ 0.052 and 0.0046 at the four ζ control points; at the campaign's twelve (δ, t) points with L = L_sign(δ, t) the ℓ¹ norm exceeds the orbit signal 2δ²c(δL)² by 8.4 to 1.3·10⁵. **CONSEQUENCE.** Any bound |P_X(g_t)| ≤ E valid uniformly over a window of length ≥ H₀ — in particular any height-uniform estimate, conditional on any hypothesis (Lindelöf, zero-density, RH itself on the prime side) or not — has E ≥ sup_𝕋|Φ| ≫ signal/2; no such estimate certifies the sign of W at a chosen height; the detector is an EVALUATION, and its cost is the term count e^{L}. **THE TWO CONTROLS (V.4), both run before any number was quoted:** the evaluation reproduces the zero-side datum of ζ at (85.7, 10), (85.7, 20), (10⁶, 10), (10⁶, 20) to 2.3·10⁻¹⁶, 6.9·10⁻¹⁷, 3.1·10⁻¹¹, 1.6·10⁻¹² (silent, all four positive; `verify/out/controls_table.json`), and it FIRES on Davenport–Heilbronn coefficient-side — Λ_DH from the log-derivative of Σa(n)n^{−s}, the four exact witnesses of `results/c3-r/m0-axiom-note.md` §6.1 reproduced first — with W_DH = −0.269812 at (85.7, 10) (8 562 nonzero coefficients) and −0.748231 at (85.7, 20) (1.21·10⁸ nonzero coefficients; the exact counts are `m6-rung1-note.md` §1–§2, the f64 program summed 15 346 and 3.4·10⁸ terms), the record's zero-side −0.270 / −0.748 (`results/c2-m2/verify/dh_negative_control_run.log`). **THE COST LINE (C), measured on this machine (Apple M4, `verify/cost_line.py`):** 181 ns per term and 2.05 ns per sieved integer single-threaded, speed-up 4.9 on 8 threads; class A (≤ 1 h) for L ≤ 28.35 (X ≤ 2.0·10¹²), class B (≤ 24 h) to L = 31.61 (X = 5.3·10¹³), class C beyond; at the campaign's L_sign table: class A at every point with δ ≥ 0.1 (seconds), class C at δ = 0.05 for t ∈ {10³, 10⁶} at the single-t bandwidth (1.6 and 5.2 days; the ensemble medians A and B). **THE WALL'S NAME (repair 6):** the Lindelöf lock (`directions/A4-lindelof-lock.md`; named at IV.6 and §0 item 5(b); the Schatten-3 route to it decided at IV.7's Session-6 closure; prime-side form at `results/full-map.md` line 203) and the campaign residue's t-averaging are the SAME wall seen from the estimate side; under a Lindelöf-strength hypothesis what improves is the t-averaged statement (a proportion o(1) of the zeros in [T, 2T] lie at depth ≥ δ, so the datum at a height sampled from the zeros misses a deep orbit with probability o(1) — III.1's territory, claimed as such), not the pointwise value; the evaluation route goes around the lock and has its own wall at the term count. The single-defect regime — one height, one evaluation of e^{L_sign(δ,t)} terms, decision by the sign — is a computation with a cost curve, not a theorem.
- **KILLS.** Any brief proposing to certify the SILENCE (or the firing) of a first-order datum at a specific height by a t-uniform prime-side estimate — the M6 "estimate route" as drafted in C2 line 48 ("short Euler-product sums P_X(g_t) … quantify required vs attainable accuracy") and every Lindelöf-conditional variant of it; any brief pricing the prime-side evaluation by the theorem's L* (e^{403}, e^{1267}) rather than by the sign channel's L_sign (the record's IV.9 rider sentence "not computable" is correct for certifying the theorem's BOUND from the prime side and is superseded for the SIGN by this entry's cost line); any brief quoting "2√X‖g‖_∞" as the size of the prime sum (the sharp trivial bound is the ℓ¹ norm Σ_n|w_n|, 7–15 times smaller at L = 10–20, and it is attained).
- **EXECUTABLE TEST.** Given a brief with a prime-side estimate E(t, L) for P_X(g_t) claimed uniform over heights: compute Σ_p max_θ φ_p(θ) from the brief's own coefficients (`verify/kronecker_instance.py` on the `small_prime_powers` list of `twisted_sum.rs`; the PNT approximation (2/L²)∫₀¹e^{Lv/2}|A(v)|dv suffices above L = 20: it is 1.4 % high at L = 10 — 1.4778 against the exact 1.4569 — and 0.02 % high at L = 20 — 12.5425 against 12.5397) and compare with the signal 2δ²c(δL)² the brief needs to resolve; if E < sup, the estimate is false on a set of heights of lower density ≥ c₀/4 > 0 in every window of length ≥ H₀ (Theorem (E), Step 5, the measure remark), and the brief is returned. Given a brief with a detection claim at height t and depth δ: read its L_sign(δ, t) against the campaign's table (CAMPAIGN.md §2) and its term count e^{L_sign} against the class boundaries above; a claim in class C without a hardware line is returned. Negative control (V.4): the channel must and does fire on DH coefficient-side at L = 10; positive control: it must and does stay silent on ζ at (85.7, 10) with the prime side matching the zero side to 10⁻¹⁴ or better.

Labels: the theorem (E) **`[classical: cited at the page]`** (Turán 1960, Lemma p. 313; Weber 2009, Theorem 1, (3.17)–(3.18)); the instance, both controls and the cost line **`computationally-verified` (dual-model, independent implementation)**; the reading of (E) as the M6 estimate wall **`[novelty: dual-model check 2026-09-17]`** — an interpretation, not a theorem; only the coefficient system and that reading are the program's. **BINDS: the M6 detector's ESTIMATE route (any t-uniform prime-side bound, conditional or not); the pricing of first-order detectors by their prime-side term count.** Does NOT bind: the evaluation route (class A/B by the cost line), the zero-side theorem M2 and its campaign, mean-value statements over windows (III.1's regime, claimed as shared), anything about where ζ's zeros are.
