## GROUP V — PROCESS BARRIERS (how briefs die for non-mathematical reasons)

### V.1 The brief-time rule (this suite's own application law)

- **STATEMENT.** A1, B1, C1 all died POST-design-cost to model checks executable at brief time; A2 died to a trace-index audit and one exact counterexample, both brief-time-executable. The suite exists to move those deaths before the spend.
- **TEST.** No design work is commissioned until the protocol (§0) is on file with, for every barrier hit, an explicit evasion argument. The critic's checklist output (barrier-by-barrier: pass / evaded-with-argument / HIT) is attached to the brief and versioned with it.
- **SOURCE.** completeness-critic.json rec 4 (via STATUS Phase 4); adjudications A1/B1/C1/A2.
- **STATUS.** program rule (binding, this file). **BINDS: all briefs.**

### V.2 The prior-art gate (novelty is checked online, not recalled)

- **STATEMENT.** Standing order 1. Program casualties of skipped/late prior art: A2's novelty claim 5 punctured by Lagarias–Rodgers (corpus w-09, "on the title alone"); the 0.6725→0.6818 "gap" pre-shrunk by Chirre–Gonçalves–de Laat 0.6792 (w-08); B3's 70.37% record competitors (w-10/w-11). Novelty checks are not optional and not from memory; paywalled/unreachable sources are reported to the sponsor, never skipped silently.
- **TEST.** Every brief carries a prior-art section with tier-1 pins checked THIS session (arXiv/zbMATH/corpus); the routing index (`results/corpus-routing.md`) is consulted for on-disk pre-emptions; MathSciNet is permanently closed — use zbMATH (routing caveat 13).
- **SOURCE.** STATUS standing order 1; corpus-routing.md; STATUS carry-forward flag (i).
- **STATUS.** sponsor standing order. **BINDS: all briefs.**
- **[RIDER 2026-09-16, Session 22 — from V.5.]** A null prior-art result is a fact about the literature and never a ground against a branch — see V.5. The gate exists to prevent duplicated work and to catch pre-emption, not to price a corner as closed.

### V.3 Verification standards (recall is not evidence; single verdicts have variance)

- **STATEMENT.** Standing order 5: no decisive action on unverified recalled claims — load-bearing mathematics is re-derived or computed, contested claims adjudicated by computation (this caught A2's F1/F2 "decisively and cheaply, ~5 min of numerics"). Session-4 process learning: single-run critic verdicts have real variance — run-2's fresh killer flipped A2 from swr-consensus to refuted; run-1's referee had computed the SHADOW of the error (0.74) inside the false formula without recognizing it. Consequently: (a) killer verdicts on load-bearing directions are DUPLICATED (two independent killers); (b) a "survives" verdict is only as good as the deepest identity actually re-derived — critics re-derive the central identity, never audit around it; (c) adjudicators verify computationally.
- **TEST.** Brief-time barrier evasion arguments are held to the same standard: an evasion resting on a recalled theorem is flagged and verified before the brief proceeds; every numeric barrier check in this file cites its reproducible computation path.
- **Session-6 amendment (C3 adjudication, binding):** **[V:scout] tags on PROVABILITY assessments are [RU] until independently re-derived.** A scout's verification of what a source SAYS is not verification of what can be PROVED: both C3 fatals traced to scout-[V]-tagged claims ("Part B is provable today"; the seed's viability). Corollary from the same cycle: referee silence is not rebuttal — referee scores are weighted by examination coverage, and the correct synthesis of a split cycle is killers' fatals + referee's additions, never an average of scores. Second corollary: "corpus sufficient for X" claims must name the exact volume and be spot-checked at commissioning (the q-13a conflation — 2019 norms/heights LNM vs the 2021 intersection-theory Mémoire — would have sent Part-A design to a book without the needed theory). Third: self-instrumented briefs convert instead of dying (C3's Z5 rule/R8 pricing/kill-criteria turned two fatals into a reduced recommission where C1 got refutation) — Track-C briefs are REQUIRED to carry conversion machinery.
- **SOURCE.** STATUS standing order 5 + findings log 2026-08-13; adjudication-A2.json (adjudication_type); adjudication-C3.json (process_findings).
- **STATUS.** sponsor standing order + program protocol. **BINDS: all briefs and all critics.**
- **[RIDER 2026-09-24, Session 24 (digest §C.3 item 5), entered at the Session-25 zoo stream — a scale-blind acceptance criterion (`results/c2-m6/m6-rung1-note.md` §5.3; `check-O.md` §3, CONFIRMED independently at 40 digits; the record corrections at `results/c2-m2/campaign/CAMPAIGN.md` and `separation-note.md`, 00:36 IST 2026-09-17, and the Session-23 re-runs).]** The on-line zeros of the Davenport–Heilbronn function in the M2 negative control and in the campaign's DH scans were located as sign changes of Z_DH(u) = Re Ξ_DH(½ + iu) refined by `mpmath.findroot` (Illinois) at 15 digits, and accepted on the checker's criterion max |Z_DH(γ)| = 1.9·10⁻³² (`results/c2-m2/SHARED.md` checkpoint (5)). That criterion is scale-blind: Ξ_DH(½ + iu) carries the factor |Γ(¾ + iu/2)| ≈ e^{−πu/4} ≈ 10⁻²⁰ … 10⁻³³ across the window, so an absolute stopping test |f| < tol is met after ONE secant step from the 0.05-wide bracket and certifies nothing — the returned points are off by 1.2·10⁻⁶ to 1.1·10⁻³ over the 36 recorded points of the ±30 window (up to 1.4·10⁻³ in the ±60 window), with |f_DH(½ + iu)| there 4.9·10⁻⁶ to 3.7·10⁻³ while |Z_DH| ≤ 4.3·10⁻²². The criterion must be |f_DH(½ + iγ)|, or the root find must run on the rescaled S(u) = Z_DH(u)/[(5/π)^{3/4}|Γ(¾ + iu/2)|], which is O(1) away from zeros: refined so at 40 digits, max |f_DH(½ + iγ)| over the 75 on-line zeros is 3.9·10⁻²⁹ and the coefficient-minus-zero residual of the M6 identity collapses to 10⁻¹⁴. Blast radius, confined: every record script forms W_Z = W(orbit ∪ on-line) and W_{Z′} = W(on-line), so W_Z − W_{Z′} = W(orbit) identically and the separation, ratio and "fires" columns of every DH control table are independent of the on-line positions (×6.24 → 6.2373, a fourth-digit move from truncating L*, not from the zeros); the W_{Z′} columns move at 10⁻⁴ relative (0.0048010832 → 0.0048001176 at L = 10) and by 6.9·10⁻³ at L* (3.4322·10⁻¹⁰ → 3.4084·10⁻¹⁰, good to two digits); the 26 off-line orbits, refined on f_DH itself, and every ζ-side number (from `zetazero`) are untouched. Standard added to (c) above: an acceptance criterion for a computed zero is stated in a quantity that is O(1) away from zeros, never as an absolute tolerance on a function that is exponentially small along the whole line — and it was the second, independent route (the coefficient side of the M6 channel, which fired before the zero side was corrected) that caught it.
- **[READ 2026-09-24, Opus 5: two precisions to the rider above, both carried from its records; the verdict "scale-blind" is strengthened, not weakened.]** (1) The measured value 1.9·10⁻³² (1.857e-32) is printed in `results/c2-m2/SHARED.md` checkpoint (5) as "Max |Z_DH(gamma)| over the builder's 39 on-line points at 25 digits", beside the campaign's t = 114.16 control row, not over the M2 control's 36 points at t = 85.7; the recipe (sign changes of Z_DH refined by `findroot` on Z_DH at 15 digits) is the same in all three scripts (`results/c2-m6/check-O.md` §3, "Which record files the correction touches"). (2) "≈ 10⁻²⁰ … 10⁻³³ across the window" is carried verbatim from `check-O.md` §3; recomputed for this read over the recorded points u = 57.04 … 112.38 of the ±30 window at 85.7, e^{−πu/4} runs 3.5·10⁻²⁰ … 4.7·10⁻³⁹ and |Γ(¾ + iu/2)| runs 2.0·10⁻¹⁹ … 3.2·10⁻³⁸ (≈ 10⁻³⁰ at u ≈ 87, as `m6-rung1-note.md` §5.3 says), and over the ±30 window at 114.16 e^{−πu/4} runs 2.0·10⁻²⁹ … 6.7·10⁻⁵⁰ (`results/zoo-s25/verify-O/r4_gamma_scale_run.log`). The factor is smaller than printed at the upper end of the window, so an absolute tolerance certifies even less there.

### V.4 The negative-control rule (a falsification test is trusted only after it has fired on a case known to fail and stayed silent on a case known to hold) — NEW, Session 21 (extracted from B2's Phase-4 finding, Session 3)

- **STATEMENT.** B2's Phase-4 cycle (2026-08-11, Session 3) found its designated "genuine falsification channel" INVERTED. Experiment 1 predicted a depth-blindness signature — the exclusion frontiers at injected depths δ = 10⁻¹ and δ = 10⁻⁶ coincide — and named non-coincidence as the falsifier of the first theorem. But depth-blindness is a property of the certificate's PRICING (Sylvester inertia charges any off-line pair n₊(Q) ≤ 1 regardless of depth), NOT of the measured functionals: the zero-side Frobenius norm that Experiment 1(ii) measures contains each injected pair's self-term of size ~ X^{|2β−1|} = e^{2Lδ}, so the two curves would not coincide even for a correct theorem, and the channel "would produce a false 'falsification' of a correct theorem" (referee) — "the designated 'genuine falsification channel' would fire on a true theorem" (killer). The referee also found the experiment's dimension estimate wrong by about 5× (d ≈ 20 600 at θ = 0.9, T = 5·10⁴). Both critics found it independently; "No conflict — no adjudication needed"; mandatory repair (2) rewrote the experiment (compare depths both in the e^{2Lδ} = 1 + o(1) regime, or subtract the exact X^{2δ} self-terms; separate the moment-visible regime from an exact-arithmetic inertia computation, which IS depth-uniform for every δ > 0). Companion exhibit already in this file: A1's M0 kill-switch was "miscalibrated in the unsafe direction" and could not fire inside the deceptive dip (IV.3, IV.9). **THE RULE.** A falsification test, kill-switch or decision gate is trusted only after it has been run on both controls: it must FIRE on the negative control (a case known to fail — an RH-false world, a known-wrong theorem, an injected defect) and stay SILENT on the positive control (a case known to hold — the true zeros in a regime where the claim is a theorem, or a model where the claim is proved). A test never run on its controls is not a test; it is a hypothesis about a test. A channel that would fire on the positive control is inverted, and a brief whose channel has not been exercised on both controls has no falsification channel at all — whatever its prose says.
- **TEST.** Every brief's falsification channel names both controls and the run that exercised them (script, parameters, output on disk), and the critic checks that the channel fired on the negative and stayed silent on the positive BEFORE any experimental milestone is credited. Both controls must sit in the detector's visible regime (IV.9): a control run below the visibility threshold proves nothing about the channel. A brief that offers a falsifier without its two control runs is RETURNED, not refuted.
- **SOURCE.** `directions/B2-refutation-program.md`, "Verification verdicts (Phase 4)" (killer: survives-with-repairs 6.5, majors incl. "Experiment 1's depth-blindness signature is mathematically wrong — the designated falsification channel would fire on a correct theorem"; referee: survives-with-repairs 7, "Independently confirmed Experiment 1's inversion (+~5x cost error)"; "No conflict — no adjudication needed. Consensus: survives-with-repairs, ~6.75") and "Mandatory repairs (merged)" (2); `results/verdicts.json`, B2 slots — referee lens (finding "Experiment 1's central falsification test (depth-uniformity of the exclusion frontier) is logically inverted and would produce a false 'falsification' of a correct theorem; its cost estimate is also wrong by ~5x", repairs (1)) and killer lens (finding "Experiment 1's core deliverable is miscalibrated: the predicted depth-blindness signature … is mathematically wrong, so the designated 'genuine falsification channel' would fire on a true theorem", with the argument from the e^{2Lδ} self-term). A1: `results/adjudication-A1.json` (kill-switch finding), IV.3 here.
- **STATUS.** program rule, extracted from a Phase-4 FINDING — not from a kill: the record's verdict on B2 is survives-with-repairs (consensus ~6.75), and this entry corrects the Session-21 brief's phrase "Phase-4 kill" at the page. The mathematical finding is dual-confirmed (killer and referee independently, Session 3); the rule is this file's, in the vocabulary of V.1–V.3 (a process barrier, not a mathematical one). **BINDS: all briefs and all critics.**
- **[RIDER 2026-09-24, Session 24 (digest §C.3 item 4), entered at the Session-25 zoo stream — a stop condition restated after it fired (`results/c2-m2/campaign/CHECK-O.md` §6 and §6.1; `CAMPAIGN.md` §1 as corrected by its Opus corrections item 4; placed on V.4 rather than V.1 because a stop condition is a decision gate — this entry's object — while V.1 governs the brief-time protocol, not gates).]** The campaign's stop condition (iv), as PRICING §2(e) wrote it, compares the density model log(t/2π)·‖B′‖₂²/L³ with the measured on-line noise N_Z at a single height t and fires when they differ by more than a factor 10. In that form it FIRED: 10 of the 59 grid bandwidths at t = 10³ (25 of 59 at 10⁵, 11 of 59 at 10⁶) lie outside a factor 10 of the model, geometric means 0.42, 2.42, 0.13, 0.28 at t = 10³ … 10⁶, the single-t N_Z/model as low as 0.0007 at t = 10⁵. The builder restated the rule on the mean over the 143–149 centers — legitimately as mathematics (the model is an ensemble mean by construction, PRICING §2(a)(4): the zero sum replaced by an integral against the mean density; at L ≳ 20 the kernel's width ≈ 5/L is far below the mean zero gap, so a single-t N_Z is one draw from a broad distribution) — and in that form it does not fire at any height ([0.42, 1.08], [0.89, 1.16], [0.69, 1.04], [0.91, 1.30]). The checker's verdict, verbatim: "legitimate as mathematics, a weakening as a stop condition, and under-recorded in CAMPAIGN.md" — "A stop condition exists to be fixed in advance; changing it on the evidence it was meant to gate is the failure mode it guards against, and the campaign then ran three more heights under the changed rule." Two findings are banked: (a) **at a single height and bandwidth the density model does not predict N_Z within a factor 10; it is off by up to a factor 1400 low at t = 10⁵** — a measured property of the instrument, not a nuisance; (b) **a stop condition restated after it fired is a weakening as a stop rule**, whatever the mathematics of the restatement. The closes are not damaged (close 1 is about thresholds in L, far more stable than the level N_Z; close 2 quotes both the center-mean and the single-t ranges), and the builder did not hide it (the literal verdict is printed in `logs/height_t1e3.log`, the `single_t_*` keys are in every `summary_<tag>.json`). Reading for THE RULE above: a gate's literal form, if it fires, is recorded as fired, and its restatement is a new gate declared before the next run, not a re-reading of the old one after the fact.

### V.5 The Grossmann-condition rule (absence of literature is never evidence against a branch) — NEW, Session 22 (extracted from the standing-order-6 refinement audit of the closed Grossmann sweep, Session 21)

- **STATEMENT.** Standing order 6's refinement (STATUS.md line 34; adopted by the orchestrator 2026-09-09, on the sponsor's observation): "every Grossmann-style scout judges fit against S1–S5 only; the absence of any published connection between a branch and ζ is never evidence against it — that absence is the Grossmann condition, not a defect. A scout that discards a branch for 'no RH literature' has misread the order." (STATUS prints the inner phrase with double quotation marks; the single marks here are nesting only.) The sponsor's framing, recorded on the same line and cited here as that framing rather than as history verified elsewhere: in 1912 no literature connected gravitation to Riemannian geometry; Grossmann matched Einstein's REQUIREMENT (metric = field, general covariance) against a branch on its internal properties. A sweep verdict, dead-end certificate, or brief-time return that rests on a null literature search — "zero papers", "nobody has", "never been applied", "no candidate exists", "no author names X as a target" — is a Grossmann-order misreading, and the branch must be re-scouted on internal properties.
- **THE DISTINCTION THIS RULE TURNS ON.** "The branch does not contain the object" is INTERNAL and admissible: it is an S4 statement about the mathematics (the square of Spec Z is unbuilt; no cohomology with the required coefficients exists; the generator has no instantiation in the class). "No one has connected the branch to ζ" is ABSENCE and inadmissible, whatever else the sentence contains. Where both are welded into one sentence — the pattern found in `results/grossmann-sweep2.json` `closure.wave2_summary[1]`, the beyond-endoscopy line, quoted in full in SOURCE — the sentence is split and only the internal half is weighed.
- **THE SIGNATURE TO MATCH (from the audit).** Generator conceded to exist as mathematics and to be non-Weil (S4 ≥ 2), every object the branch has actually built killed on internal grounds, and the unbuilt sector closed by a sentence about publications. Three sweep rows carry it (`results/sweep-audit-s21/AUDIT.md` §3 and §4 observation 3; verdict and confidence re-read from `results/grossmann-sweep.json` at insertion): lorentzian-log-concavity (`reports[14]`, dead-end 0.82, zoo III.16 — see its 2026-09-16 rider), condensed-analytic-stacks (`reports[25]`, instrument 0.60), lee-yang-stat-mech (`reports[26]`, instrument 0.72). The last two have no zoo entry and no cross-reference row; they are named here and nowhere else in this file.
- **EXECUTABLE TEST.** Strike every absence-of-literature sentence from the verdict, the dead-end reason, or the brief-time return. Does the conclusion still follow from what remains — a model-world kill, a proven reparametrization, an o(N) or ensemble-level argument, an equivalence, or a no-go in print? If yes, the verdict stands. If no, the verdict is returned for re-scouting under S1–S5 on internal properties, and the branch's residual value may not be recorded as closed.
- **WHAT THIS RULE DOES NOT SAY.** It changes NO verdict by itself. The three named rows keep their recorded grades (dead-end 0.82; instrument 0.60; instrument 0.72) until a re-scout on internal properties reports; whether such a re-scout is a decidable unit is decided by the Session 22 pricing stream (queue item 0(b)(ii), output to land at `results/grossmann-rescout-s22/PRICING.md` — queued, not yet written, at this entry's insertion). Nothing here says that any of the three branches fits S1–S5 (AUDIT §5: "their exclusions are not fully supported by the grounds on record, and under the refinement the grounds on record are all they may rest on"). V.5 governs how verdicts may be GROUNDED, not what they are.
- **SOURCE.** STATUS.md line 34 (standing order 6 + the 2026-09-09 refinement); `results/sweep-audit-s21/AUDIT.md` (Opus 5, 2026-09-10; SHA-256 `13dd2abfb49a4c6953b07916d9ec4da685759354c16c13668ea00d32d872e4dc`; 38 reports classified — A 9 / B 26 / C 3 / D 0, recounted at insertion from the 38 `###` rows of §2 and their `CLASSIFICATION:` lines); `results/grossmann-sweep.json` `reports[14]`, `reports[25]`, `reports[26]` (fields `verdict`, `confidence`, read at insertion); `results/grossmann-sweep2.json` `closure.wave2_summary[1]`, verbatim: "beyond-endoscopy-ngo — INSTRUMENT 0.85. Decisive: verified-negative — zero papers ever couple beyond endoscopy to RH, and the branch's target outputs (FE + continuation + pole at s=1) are exactly the DH/Epstein-shared properties; but monoid membership consumes multiplicativity at the axiom level (S1:4), and the Blomer–Leung converse mechanism (Adv. Math. 2026) is the sharpest formal home for the DH/Epstein exclusion lemma — feeds wave-1 shortlist #4 role (a)." — the absence half before the semicolon, the internal half after it, in one string.
- **STATUS.** program rule (process barrier, in the vocabulary of V.1–V.4; extracted from an AUDIT finding, not from a kill). **BINDS: all Grossmann-style scouts, all dead-end certificates, and all brief-time returns.**
- **[READ 2026-09-16, Opus 5: one clause of the SIGNATURE line is REPAIRED.]** “The last two have no zoo entry and no cross-reference row; they are named here and nowhere else in this file” is exact for condensed-analytic-stacks but not for lee-yang-stat-mech: III.15’s SOURCE line already names “lee-yang-stat-mech instrument 0.72” by slug, and III.15’s KILLS line already names “the lee-yang feasibility brief” as the dark-horse exception on file (“precisely an attempt to satisfy this test, not evade it”). Read the clause as: neither branch has a zoo entry of its own and neither has a cross-reference-table row, and the slug `condensed-analytic-stacks` occurs in this file only here. The entries the audit names as binding the two branches at brief time are III.14, III.17, III.20, III.21, IV.1, I.1, I.7 and IV.10 for condensed-analytic-stacks, and III.15, III.6, I.1, I.5, III.16, III.20 and IV.9 for lee-yang-stat-mech (`results/sweep-audit-s21/AUDIT.md` §3.2, §3.3). Nothing in the rule or its grade turns on the clause; the three verdict/confidence pairs, the class counts A 9 / B 26 / C 3 / D 0, the STATUS line-34 quotation and the audit SHA-256 were all re-read independently at the record and CONFIRMED.
- **[READ 2026-09-16, Opus 5: the SOURCE line’s split description is REPAIRED.]** “the absence half before the semicolon, the internal half after it, in one string” is not exact. Re-read at the record (`results/grossmann-sweep2.json` `closure.wave2_summary[1]`, a list of 11 strings; the quotation above is verbatim and occurs once in this file): the clause before the semicolon carries internal content too — “the branch’s target outputs (FE + continuation + pole at s=1) are exactly the DH/Epstein-shared properties” is a statement about the mathematics, and AUDIT §4 observation 1 records that the scout’s own report (WP-01) “contains the internal half and not the absence half”. The absence content is the single clause “zero papers ever couple beyond endoscopy to RH”. The split this rule prescribes is therefore per clause, not at the semicolon; the instruction itself — split the sentence, weigh only the internal half — is unchanged.

The closed-3-manifold finite-rank length-group theorem (IV.13, B1′ as corrected in `f1-check-O.md` §6.4): a finite-rank statement about H₁ — dim_Q(Λ_{M₀} ⊗ Q) ≤ dim_Q H₁(M ∖ N; Q), the Mayer–Vietoris step dim H₁(W ∖ L) ≤ dim H₁(W) + dim H₁(L), and the Q-linear independence of {log p} from unique factorization. Lean-amenable in principle: the arithmetic half (independence of {log p}; a finite-rank subgroup of R cannot contain a Q-independent infinite set) is elementary and kernel-checkable; the topological half needs a Mayer–Vietoris sequence for open covers of a 3-manifold and must be checked against Mathlib's current homology coverage before commissioning. Statement must carry the tangency-set N, transverse orbits only, and "finite rank" (never "finitely generated").

The fractional-mark integrality theorem (IV.17): a finite statement over integer marks — Lemma 2.2 of `theorems.md` (per atom, (m − 1)(m − 2) ≥ 0 and 1_{m=1} ≥ 2m − m², equality iff m ∈ {1, 2}), the master inequality (MI) F1 ≥ 3M − 2N_d on the grid class via grid Parseval, and the two corners as its consequences — together with its NEGATION over rational marks: the mark-4/3 atom configuration (N = 64: 48 atoms of mark 4/3 on distinct grid sites; F1 = Σ m² = 256/3 = (4/3)·64, N_d = 48, 3M − 2N_d = 96 > 256/3) is a kernel-checkable certificate that (MI) fails for fractional marks. Lean-amenable in the GridParseval style: `GridCorner.lean` already carries Lemma 2.2(b) (`mark_one_count_ge`) and the corner bounds; the counterexample needs the grid-Parseval identity stated with RATIONAL marks (it is linear algebra, so the extension is mechanical — check the Lean typing of marks before commissioning) and one exact rational evaluation (`decide +kernel`). The pair-channel form (Prop. 4.5: F1 − S2 = 2μ²ā(2d)² − 4μ(ā(d)² − 1) with ā a cosh sum) is a real-analytic identity plus one Cauchy–Schwarz step — paper-certificate grade, or Lean only with interval arithmetic on ā; the numeric anchor (d, μ) = (0.25, 0.05), F1 − S2 = −3.520·10⁻², is the check.
